- Next: detail routing on mult24 and mult32; a causal fanout test on `fanao96`.

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 71: a gate that costs no routing, R2 against R3 on six placed probes (predictions held in my notes before the run, not written to this log first; no flow)
- Why: milestone 70's "Next" asked for a gate cheaper than six routed probes. `experiments/bohb/gate3.py` (a copy of `gate.py`) places m = 6 random points to R3 (charged R2 + R3 each, not eligible as answers, not fed to the KDE), takes Kendall tau-b between their R2 and R3 scores, and falls back to BOHB-R4-first on the remaining budget if tau < 0.4, else BOHB. Same 48 cells, 100 paired seeds, same settings; m and threshold not tuned.
- Predictions (made before running, recorded here after): the gate does not beat plain BOHB at 40 equivalents by 0.5 points, and it falls back on fewer than half the seeds in the failing multiplier cells.
- **Outcome (`gate3_main.txt`, `gate3_unfaithful.txt`, `gate3_unfaithful2.txt` and `.json`; my arithmetic pooled over the 48 cells).** Mean regret % at 15 / 25 / 40: random 3.41 / 1.67 / 0.35; BOHB 2.39 / 2.17 / 1.62; BOHB-R4-first 2.79 / 1.31 / 0.63; **R3 gate 3.81 / 2.60 / 1.98**. Eight relaxed multiplier cells at 40: BOHB 5.88, gate 5.31, random 0.33. The gate is worse than BOHB in 34 of 40 main cells at every budget (sign test p < 0.001) and falls back on only 7 to 18% of seeds in the multiplier cells, about the same as elsewhere (12 to 16%).
- Reading: negative. R3 (placement) inherits R2's ordering; the multiplier failure comes from timing repair at routing, which neither rung sees, so R2-versus-R3 agreement carries no signal about R2-versus-R4 faithfulness. Together with milestone 70, no gate tried beats the simple rule: at 25 or more routed-run equivalents do not cut on R2 first.
- Limits: m = 6 and threshold untuned; one family of failing cells; the multiplier cells are in-sample for the idea.
- Next: carry milestones 68 to 71 into the paper.

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 70: an R4-probe gate that falls back to routing-first when R2 disagrees with a few routed probes (predictions written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestones 68 and 69 left BOHB stuck at 6.5 to 8.5% regret on the relaxed multiplier cells, where random routing and BOHB-R4-first converge. The "Next" asked for a gate. `experiments/bohb/gate.py` replays the cached tables (40 main cells, mult20 and mult28, mult36 and mult40: 48 cells; the main cells include mult24 and mult32), 100 paired seeds, the milestone 27 BOHB settings.
- Arms (budgets 15, 25, 40 routed-run equivalents; the probes are charged and eligible as answers): `gate`: route m = 6 random points at R4, compute Kendall tau-b between their R2 scores and routed scores; if tau < 0.4 run BOHB-R4-first on the remaining budget, else BOHB (R2 first); `probe_bohb` and `probe_r4first`: the same probe spend with no gate (controls that separate the cost of the probes from the value of the decision); plain `bohb`, `bohb_r4first` and random routing from milestone 69's harness. Probes are not fed to the KDE (conservative). Threshold 0.4 and m = 6 are fixed now, not tuned.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) at 15 equivalents the gate's mean regret over the 48 cells is above plain BOHB's (six probes are 40% of the budget); (2) at 40 equivalents the gate's mean regret is below plain BOHB's by at least 0.5 points; (3) on the six relaxed multiplier cells the gate's mean regret at 40 is below 3% (BOHB: about 6); (4) the gate misfires (falls back where BOHB was fine) on at least 20% of non-multiplier cells, because tau over six points is noisy.
- Limits known in advance: probe count and threshold untuned and a six-point tau is noisy; the six multiplier cells were the reason for the gate, so their result is in-sample evidence about the mechanism, and the other 42 cells are the check on its cost.

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/gate.py`; `gate_main.txt`, `gate_unfaithful.txt`, `gate_unfaithful2.txt` and the `.json` cells; 48 cells, 100 paired seeds, replay about 2.5 min; the pooled means below are my arithmetic over the three files).** Mean regret % over all 48 cells at 15 / 25 / 40 equivalents: random 3.41 / 1.67 / 0.35; BOHB 2.39 / 2.17 / 1.62; BOHB-R4-first 2.79 / 1.31 / 0.63; probes then BOHB 3.18 / 1.42 / 1.29; probes then R4-first 2.95 / 1.35 / 0.46; **gate 3.14 / 1.42 / 1.16**. On the eight relaxed multiplier cells at 40: BOHB 5.88, gate 3.27, probes then R4-first 0.53, random 0.33. Per failing cell at 40, BOHB to gate: mult24 11.35 to 6.29, mult32 13.78 to 6.65, mult28 6.89 to 3.60, mult36 6.46 to 2.56, mult40 8.52 to 6.83. The gate fell back on 30 to 33% of seeds in the multiplier cells and on 21% in the other 40 cells.
  - (1) held (gate 3.14 against BOHB 2.39 at 15; the gate is worse than BOHB in 26 of 40 main cells, sign test p 0.011). (2) **refuted, narrowly:** at 40 the gate is 0.46 below BOHB pooled (1.16 against 1.62), not 0.5. (3) **refuted:** 3.27% on the eight relaxed multiplier cells, not under 3%. (4) held (21% pooled over the 40 other cells).
- Reading: a negative result for the gate as designed. It halves the tail but does not remove it, because six probes give a Kendall tau that is too noisy to tell a failing cell (tau of R2 over the whole pool 0.41 to 0.65) from a healthy one: it falls back on only a third of the failing draws and on a fifth of the healthy ones. The control that always routes first after the probes (`probe_r4first`, 0.46 at 40, 1.35 at 25) is as good as random routing at 40 and beats BOHB at 25 and 40, so on these 48 cells the practical advice is simpler than a gate: if the budget is 25 or more routed-run equivalents, do not cut on R2 first. BOHB's edge is confined to 15 equivalents (2.39 against 3.41 for random routing), where the probes cost more than the gate saves.
- Limits: m = 6 and threshold 0.4 fixed, not tuned (a larger m would cost more of a 15-equivalent budget); probes are not fed to the KDE; the multiplier cells motivated the gate, so they are in-sample; 48 cells share designs and a single 60-sequence sample per cell; pooled means are cell-weighted.
- Next: a gate on something cheaper than tau of six probes (R2 against R3 on the entrants, which costs no routing), or accept the simple rule and carry it into the paper.

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 69: how often does the multiplier family at the relaxed clock leave BOHB stuck? mult36 and mult40 (predictions written before the flow output was opened)
- Why: milestone 68 found one failing cell of four (mult28 relaxed) and its "Next" asked for more widths to count how often the family fails. `experiments/unfaithful/run.py` now takes `--widths 36 40 --results results_w36_40.jsonl`; same protocol as milestones 9 and 68 (60 sequences from seed 0, clocks 1.0x and 1.5x the baseline no-wire period, four rungs, tt corner, 40% utilisation), OpenROAD 26Q3, Yosys 0.33. `bohb.py unfaithful2` replays it with the milestone 27 settings (writes `results_unfaithful2.txt`).
- **Predictions on record:** (1) BOHB at 40 equivalents has regret above 3% on at least one of the two relaxed-clock cells (the family is the risk); (2) SH from R2 keeps more than 3% regret at 25 and 40 on at least one relaxed cell; (3) at 15 equivalents BOHB beats random routing on average over the four cells; (4) tight-clock cells are at or below 1% BOHB regret at 40.
- Limits known in advance: four cells, one family, no pooled test; a failure rate over the six widths seen so far (20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40) is a count of cells, not an estimate with an interval worth quoting.

- **Outcome (`experiments/unfaithful/results_w36_40.jsonl`: 240 flow runs, 200 ok, 40 failed in ABC, 10 per design and clock; `experiments/bohb/results_unfaithful2.txt`, `cells_unfaithful2.json`; 100 paired seeds, replay 3 s; flow about 65 min on 4 cores).** Tau(R2,R4): mult36 tight 0.74, relaxed 0.41; mult40 tight 0.83, relaxed 0.65. Mean regret % over the four cells at 15 / 25 / 40 equivalents: random 2.68 / 1.28 / 0.34; BOHB 1.83 / 3.92 / 3.92; SH from R2 3.92 at every budget; BOHB-R4-first 2.34 / 1.41 / 0.94. Both relaxed cells fail: **BOHB is stuck at 6.46 (mult36) and 8.52 (mult40) at 25 and 40 equivalents**, the same as SH from R2, while random routing reaches 0.11 and 0.87 at 40 and BOHB-R4-first 0.0 and 3.77. Tight cells: BOHB 0.34 / 0.0 / 0.0 (mult36) and 0.08 / 0.71 / 0.71 (mult40).
  - (1) held (6.46 and 8.52 against 3%). (2) held (6.46 and 8.52 at 25 and 40). (3) held (1.83 against 2.68; BOHB lower in 4 of 4 cells, sign test p 0.125, n = 4). (4) held (0.0 and 0.71).
- Reading: with milestone 68, the relaxed clock has now left the rung-cut methods stuck in three of the four relaxed cells run under 26Q3 (mult28, 36, 40; mult20 did not), and the R2-limited cell is not rare in this family; with milestone 13 (24 and 32 bits) the failing cells are most of the relaxed multiplier widths, though I did not recompute BOHB on those two. More budget does not rescue any rung-cut method there, and the failing regret grows with width (6.9, 6.5, 8.5). Random routing and BOHB-R4-first recover, so the 15-equivalent edge of BOHB (1.83 against 2.68 here) is bought with a tail that appears in a clock regime a practitioner would use (a relaxed target). A practitioner who cuts on R2 in a multiplier-heavy design at a relaxed clock should not.
- Limits: four cells, one family, no pooled test (sign test n = 4 only); tool version 26Q3 against 26Q2 in earlier tables; 40 of 240 runs fail in ABC and drop out; the same 60 sequences as milestone 68 (seed 0), so the cells share the pass-sequence sample.
- Next: the R4-probe gate that falls back to R4-first when R2's top picks disagree with one routed probe, tested on the six multiplier widths.

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 68: do BOHB and the R2 cut fail on multiplier widths never run before, where R2 is known to mis-rank? (predictions written before the flow output was opened)
- Why: the "Next" of milestones 62 to 67 was a held-out design set where R2 is unfaithful, because the iid-noise arms cannot say how BOHB behaves under structured R2 failure. The only known structured failure is the multiplier family at the relaxed clock (mult24, mult32; milestones 9, 13, 15). Two widths of that family not in any earlier table, `mult20` and `mult28` (`designs/mult_op.v`, W = 20, 28), run under the milestone 9 protocol unchanged (60 sequences from seed 0, clocks 1.0x and 1.5x the baseline no-wire period, four rungs, tt corner, 40% utilisation) as `experiments/unfaithful/run.py`; OpenROAD is 26Q3 from the nixpkgs cache (earlier tables used 26Q2, so a tool-version difference is a stated confound), Yosys 0.33. `bohb.py unfaithful` replays the table with the milestone 27 settings.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) at the relaxed clock R2 pick regret exceeds 10% on at least one of the two widths (milestone 13: 11 to 18% on 24 and 32); (2) at 15 equivalents BOHB's gain over random routing is smaller on these four cells than the 0.94 points of the 40 main cells, and negative on at least one cell; (3) at 40 equivalents random routing beats BOHB, as on the main cells; (4) mean tau(R2,R4) over the four cells is below the 0.62 of the main cells.
- Limits known in advance: two designs, four cells, one family chosen because it is the bad one, so no pooled significance test is possible, per-cell numbers only; the tool version differs from earlier tables.

- **Outcome (`experiments/unfaithful/results.jsonl`: 240 flow runs, 200 ok and 40 failed in ABC, 10 per design and clock; `experiments/bohb/results_unfaithful.txt`, `cells_unfaithful.json`; 100 paired seeds, replay about 1 min).** The first flow job was killed at 180 of 240 points by the 30-minute background limit; `run.py` now resumes from a partial `results.jsonl` and the last 60 points ran in a second job (sequences and clocks are fixed by seed and baseline, so the table is the same one). Tau(R2,R4) per cell: mult20 tight 0.75, relaxed 0.51; mult28 tight 0.76, relaxed 0.57. Mean regret % over the four cells at 15 / 25 / 40 equivalents: random 2.67 / 1.09 / 0.27; BOHB 0.65 / 1.64 / 1.72; SH from R2 1.72 at every budget; BOHB-R4-first 0.86 / 0.64 / 0.05. One cell carries it: **mult28 at the relaxed clock**, where SH from R2 keeps 6.89 points of regret at every budget (R2 cuts the best design), BOHB goes 2.02 / 6.55 / 6.89 against random 2.68 / 1.03 / 0.22, and BOHB-R4-first recovers (3.36 / 2.54 / 0.19). The other three cells are 0.00 for BOHB at 25 and 40.
  - (1) **refuted by the proxy I have:** the worst R2-limited regret is 6.9% (SH from R2 on mult28 relaxed), not above 10%; I did not compute a separate R2 pick regret, so read this as "6.9 on the closest measure". (2) **refuted on both halves:** BOHB's gain at 15 is 2.02 points on average (larger than 0.94) and positive in all 4 cells (sign test p 0.125, n = 4). (3) **held in the mean, not by cell count:** random beats BOHB at 40 on average (0.27 against 1.72), but BOHB is level or better in 3 of the 4 cells; the mean is the one failing cell. (4) **refuted:** mean tau is 0.65, not below 0.62.
- Reading: structured R2 failure is a different risk from iid noise. Tau of 0.57 at the failing cell looks ordinary, yet a rung-cut method (SH from R2, BOHB's brackets) loses the optimum for good, so more budget does not help (6.9 at 25 and 40), while random routing and BOHB-R4-first, which never discard on R2 alone, converge. For a practitioner: BOHB's low-budget edge (2.0 points here) comes with a tail the milestone 62 to 67 noise arms did not show, a cell where it is stuck at 6.9 while random routing reaches 0.2. Within two multiplier widths the failing cell is one of four, consistent with 24 and 32 (the family at the relaxed clock), so the family, not one width, is the risk.
- Limits: two designs, four cells, family chosen as the bad one; tool version 26Q3 against 26Q2 in earlier tables (not separated); 40 of 240 runs fail in ABC and drop out; 4 cells allow no useful interval.
- Tooling: OpenROAD 26Q3 comes from the nixpkgs cache with `nix build --impure --expr '(import (builtins.getFlake "nixpkgs") {system="x86_64-linux";}).openroad'` (about 3 min in a cloud session). Overriding the package (as `research/chip-pnr/README.md` shows with `doCheck=false`) forces a source build and does not finish in 25 minutes; use the plain package. Earlier notes that cloud sessions lack OpenROAD are out of date.
- Next: with OpenROAD available, a gate that falls back to R4-first when R2's top picks disagree with a cheap routed probe; more widths of the multiplier family at the relaxed clock to count how often the cell fails (mult36, mult40).

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 67: paired tests for the score-only, features-only and both-corrupted arms (no flow; predictions not written before the run)
- Why: milestone 66 tested the no-R2 arm against random routing by a paired bootstrap over the 40 cells; the arms of milestones 62 and 63 had only means. Reran `degrade.py` for `BOHB_PART=score|features|both` (100 paired seeds, about 12 min with three in parallel, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`); the `both` rerun reproduced `degrade.txt` byte for byte (git shows it unchanged), which checks the harness. New files: `degrade_paired_score.txt`, `degrade_paired_features.txt`, `degrade_paired.txt`.
- **Outcome (BOHB minus random, points of regret, bootstrap over 40 cells; negative = BOHB better).** At 15 equivalents: faithful R2 -0.94 [-1.72, -0.19]; score only sigma 0.3 / 1 -0.79 [-1.48, -0.14] / -0.83 [-1.48, -0.21]; features only -0.99 [-1.73, -0.31] / -0.63 [-1.24, -0.04]; both sigma 0.1 / 0.3 / 1 -0.98 / -0.76 / -0.16 [-0.76, +0.45]. At 25: every interval includes 0. At 40: BOHB is worse than random in every arm, +0.76 to +1.25, intervals exclude 0 (faithful R2 +1.01 [+0.22, +2.06]).
- Reading: the 15-equivalent win is statistically clear for a faithful R2 and for either single corruption (features-only sigma 1 only just: upper end -0.04), and is lost only when both channels are noise at sigma 1; this matches milestones 63 and 66. The more useful finding for a practitioner is the 40-equivalent row: BOHB loses to random at the larger budget even with a faithful R2, so it is a low-budget tool only. Caveats: 40 cells share designs; the bootstrap is descriptive; the sign test and bootstrap sometimes disagree (25 equivalents, faithful: lower in 25 of 36, p 0.029, interval includes 0), which says the means are driven by a few large cells.
- Next: unfaithful-R2 held-out designs under OpenROAD (not installed in cloud sessions).

- Added the score-only, features-only and no-R2 results to the PAPER.md limitations paragraph, quoting `degrade_*.txt` as logged, and stated that the routed-observations explanation from milestone 62 is not supported (milestone 64). The 0.23-point gap to random in the no-R2 arm still has no significance test: `bohb.py` writes only means, so a paired test needs per-seed output (next, about 8 min of replay, needs numpy).

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 66: paired test of the no-R2 arm against random routing (predictions written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestone 64 found BOHB with an uninformative R2 at 3.79 / 2.19 / 1.25 against random 3.56 / 1.77 / 0.36 (15 / 25 / 40 equivalents) and left the 0.23-point gap at 15 untested. `degrade.py` now also writes `degrade_paired*.txt`: bohb minus random per cell (each cell already a mean over 100 paired seeds, the cell is the unit), 2000-resample bootstrap interval over the 40 cells, and a two-sided sign test. Rerun of `BOHB_PART=none python3 degrade.py` (same seeds as milestone 64, so the means must reproduce `degrade_none.txt`).
- **Predictions on record:** (1) at 15 the interval for bohb minus random includes 0 (the +0.23 is noise); (2) at 25 the +0.42 interval excludes 0 or just touches it; (3) at 40 the +0.89 gap is clearly above 0 (interval excludes 0, sign test p < 0.05); (4) the means reproduce milestone 64 to two decimals.
- Limits: 40 non-independent cells (designs repeat across clocks); cell-level test ignores seed-level spread.
- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/degrade_paired_none.txt`; 40 main cells, 100 paired seeds, about 2 min; means reproduce `degrade_none.txt`).** BOHB minus random routing, points of regret: **+0.23 [-0.23, +0.71]** at 15 equivalents (lower in 18 cells, higher in 18, sign test p 1.00); **+0.42 [+0.12, +0.71]** at 25 (lower in 10, higher in 26, p 0.011); **+0.89 [+0.60, +1.19]** at 40 (lower in 2, higher in 34, p < 0.001).
  - (1) held. (2) held (the interval excludes 0, lower end +0.12). (3) held. (4) held.
- Reading: with an uninformative R2 BOHB is statistically level with random routing at 15 equivalents and reliably worse at 25 and 40. Together with milestones 62 to 64 this closes the isolation chain on iid corruption: the 15-equivalent win needs some R2 signal, and a useless R2 costs more than it saves once the budget is not tiny. The paper sentence now carries the test. Intervals are bootstrap over 40 non-independent cells (designs repeat across clocks), so read them as descriptive.
- Next: unfaithful-R2 held-out designs under OpenROAD (not installed in cloud sessions); the sigma 0 / 1 paired tables for the other arms could be run the same way (about 8 min each).

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 64: a no-R2 arm, BOHB with the R2 score and features carrying no information (predictions written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestone 63 left open whether BOHB's low-budget win comes from the routed observations and bracket structure. `bohb.py` takes `BOHB_PART=none`: every R2 score is set to a constant (ties broken at random) and the KDE features are replaced by pure N(0,1) noise; R3 and R4 scores are untouched, and R2 time is still charged. `BOHB_PART=none python3 degrade.py` replays the 40 main cells, 100 paired seeds, into `degrade_none.txt`.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) BOHB at 15 equivalents stays below random routing (3.56) but by less than 0.16 points, since milestone 62's sigma 1 (tau 0.06) already left 0.16; (2) at 25 BOHB is within 0.3 of random (1.77); (3) at 40 random still wins; (4) SH from R2 is near its worst, above 5.7 at 15.
- Limits: iid noise; cost of the unused R2 stage still paid.
- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/degrade_none.txt`; 40 main cells, 100 paired seeds, about 8 min; mean regret %, random 3.56 / 1.77 / 0.36 at 15 / 25 / 40 equivalents).** BOHB 3.79 / 2.19 / 1.25; BOHB-R4-first 4.07 / 2.02 / 0.74; SH from R2 6.06 / 4.06 / 3.62.
  - (1) **wrong:** BOHB is 0.23 points worse than random at 15, not better. (2) **wrong by a little:** at 25 BOHB is 0.42 worse than random (I said within 0.3). (3) held: random wins at 40 (0.36 vs 1.25). (4) held: SH from R2 is 6.06 at 15.
- Reading: with R2 carrying no information, BOHB's low-budget win is gone (3.79 against 3.56, a difference I did not test for significance), so the win is not explained by routed observations plus bracket structure alone. Together with milestone 63 (either R2 channel alone is enough) and 62 (tau 0.06 on both leaves 0.16 points), the edge needs some R2 signal, but very little: R2 at tau 0.06 still beats no signal by about 0.4 points. The earlier milestone 62 suggestion that routed observations carry the win is not supported; that sentence should not go in the paper.
- Limits: iid synthetic corruption; the none arm pays R2's time for nothing, as does any real BOHB with a useless R2; no significance test on the 0.23 gap.
- Next: unfaithful-R2 held-out designs under OpenROAD (needs the flow).

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 63: separate the two corruptions, R2 score only and KDE features only (no flow)
- Why: milestone 62 corrupted the R2 score and the standardised KDE features together and found BOHB kept most of its 15-equivalent win until sigma 1. Which corruption matters? `bohb.py` takes `BOHB_PART=score|features|both`; `degrade.py` with that variable replays sigma 0.3 and 1.0 (100 paired seeds, 40 main cells, about 8 min each, no flow) into `degrade_score.txt` / `degrade_features.txt`.
- **Process note:** I did not write predictions before this run (the scheduled run went straight to the replay); read the reading below as post hoc. Seeds are keyed by the run tag, so the noise draws differ from milestone 62's; the features-only sh_r2 column equals sigma 0 exactly, as it must (SH reads only the score), which checks the switch.
- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/degrade_score.txt`, `degrade_features.txt`; mean regret %, random routing 3.56 / 1.77 / 0.36 at 15 / 25 / 40 equivalents).** BOHB at 15 equivalents: sigma 0 2.62; score only 2.77 (0.3), 2.73 (1.0); features only 2.57 (0.3), 2.94 (1.0); both 2.80 (0.3), 3.40 (1.0). At 25: score only 1.76 / 1.97, features only 2.07 / 2.20, both 1.93 / 2.24. At 40 BOHB stays 1.1 to 1.6 against random 0.36 in every arm.
- Reading: either corruption alone, even at sigma 1 (R2 score tau 0.06, or features pure noise of the same size as their spread), leaves BOHB's 15-equivalent win at 0.6 to 0.8 points of 0.94; only both together reduce it to 0.16. So BOHB's low-budget edge is not carried by one R2 channel: it survives losing the R2 ranking (it still has the routed observations and the bracket structure) or losing the features (it still has the R2 scores), and disappears only when R2 is uninformative in both. That fits milestone 62's suggestion that routed observations plus Hyperband structure matter, but does not isolate them (that needs an arm with R2 removed entirely, not yet run). Differences of 0.1 to 0.2 points are within seed noise.
- Limits: iid synthetic noise; two sigma values; the score channel in BOHB feeds both bracket ranking and the KDE split and I did not separate those; the 25 and 40 results are unchanged in conclusion (random wins at 40).
- Next: a no-R2 arm (BOHB with R2 observations removed) to isolate the routed-observation contribution; unfaithful-R2 held-out designs under OpenROAD.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 62: degrade R2 on purpose and replay BOHB (predictions written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestone 61 found only a weak, interval-includes-0 correlation between R2 faithfulness and BOHB's gain. A causal test: `bohb.py` takes `BOHB_DEGRADE=sigma`, multiplying each R2 score by exp(sigma * N(0,1)) and adding N(0, sigma) to the standardised KDE features (seeded per cell; routed R4 untouched; sigma 0 reproduces `results.txt`, checked). `degrade.py` replays the 40 main cells at sigma 0, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0 and reports mean tau(R2,R4) and mean regret of random, BOHB, BOHB-R4-first and SH-from-R2 at 15, 25 and 40 equivalents.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) mean tau falls monotonically with sigma, to near 0.2 at sigma 1; (2) BOHB's 15-equivalent win over random shrinks with sigma and is gone (BOHB >= random) at sigma 1; (3) sigma 0.1 changes BOHB's regret by under 0.5 points; (4) SH from R2 degrades faster than BOHB, since BOHB mixes in random entrants and routed observations; (5) random routing is unchanged at every sigma (it never reads R2).
- Limits: the noise is synthetic and independent per point, real R2 unfaithfulness is structured; 40 cells of 10 designs.

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/degrade.txt`; 40 main cells, 100 paired seeds, about 8 min; sigma 0 reproduces `results.txt`).** Mean tau(R2,R4) over cells falls 0.62 / 0.41 / 0.22 / 0.06 for sigma 0 / 0.1 / 0.3 / 1.0. BOHB mean regret % at 15 equivalents: 2.62 / 2.59 / 2.80 / 3.40 (random routing 3.56 throughout); at 25: 2.05 / 1.81 / 1.93 / 2.24 (random 1.77); at 40: 1.38 / 1.17 / 1.13 / 1.61 (random 0.36). SH from R2 at 15: 4.93 / 4.86 / 5.13 / 5.74.
  - (1) **half wrong:** tau falls monotonically but to 0.06, not 0.2, at sigma 1. (2) **half held:** the 15-equivalent win shrinks from 0.94 to 0.16 points at sigma 1 but is not gone (3.40 < 3.56). (3) **held:** at sigma 0.1 BOHB moves 0.03 points at 15, 0.24 at 25, 0.21 at 40 (the 25 and 40 changes are in the favourable direction). (4) **wrong:** SH from R2 loses 0.81 points at 15 and BOHB 0.78: no difference in how fast they degrade. (5) held by construction.
- Reading: destroying most of R2's ranking (tau 0.62 to 0.06) costs BOHB only 0.8 of its 0.94-point low-budget win, and tau 0.41 to 0.22 costs almost nothing. So most of BOHB's advantage over random routing at 15 equivalents does not come from R2 being faithful; a plausible source (untested) is the routed observations that fit the KDE and the Hyperband bracket structure. The "R2 is faithful on the held-out designs" explanation of milestones 55 and 60 is therefore not needed and should be dropped; the held-out gain size remains unexplained. Noise is iid and synthetic, so structured real unfaithfulness (mult24, mult32 relaxed) may behave differently; that still needs OpenROAD.
- Limits: sigma 0.1 already drops tau to 0.41, so the grid has no points between 0.62 and 0.41; noise on R2's score and the KDE features at once, not separated.
- Next: separate the two corruptions (score only, features only); then the unfaithful-R2 held-out designs under OpenROAD.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 61: does BOHB's advantage over random routing track how faithful R2 is on the cell? (predictions written before the analysis; no flow)
- Why: milestones 55 and 60 explained the bigger held-out win by "R2 is faithful there" without testing it. `bohb.py` now also writes `cells.json` / `cells_unseen.json` (per-cell mean regret per arm and budget, plus the cell's Kendall tau-b of R2 against routed R4); the printed tables are unchanged (rerun reproduces `results.txt`). `faith_corr.py` takes the 50 cells and reports Spearman rho between tau(R2, R4) and the gain (random minus BOHB regret, points) at 15, 25 and 40 equivalents, with a 2000-resample bootstrap interval over cells and the same split by tight and relaxed clock.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) rho > 0 at 15 and 25 (gain rises with R2 faithfulness), interval excluding 0 at 15; (2) at 40 rho is also positive but weaker, since random is nearly saturated; (3) the correlation is driven partly by the clock (relaxed cells have higher tau and lower regret), so within each clock the rho is smaller than pooled; (4) the worst BOHB cells (barrel64, mult32) have low tau. If rho is about 0 the "faithful R2" explanation is unsupported and the paper must drop it.
- Limits: 50 non-independent cells, designs repeat across clocks; tau summarises ranking, not the top-of-ranking behaviour a KDE uses; correlation, not a causal test (that needs an R2 degraded on purpose).

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/faith_corr.txt`; 50 cells, bootstrap over cells; `bohb.py` rerun reproduces `results.txt` and `results_unseen.txt` unchanged).** Spearman rho between tau(R2, R4) and the gain over random routing is +0.19 [-0.13, +0.48] at 15 equivalents, +0.14 [-0.18, +0.44] at 25 and +0.08 [-0.24, +0.39] at 40; within a clock it is +0.12 to +0.14 at 15 (n 25, intervals about +/-0.45), and `bohb_r4first` is similar (+0.21 at 15, -0.06 at 40). Every interval includes 0.
  - (1) **half wrong:** rho is positive at 15 and 25 but the 15-equivalent interval includes 0. (2) held in sign, indistinguishable from 0. (3) **wrong:** the within-clock rho is about as large as the pooled one, so the clock does not drive it. (4) held for 4 of the 5 worst cells (barrel64 both clocks, barrel16 relaxed, alu32 tight: tau 0.17 to 0.49), not tight adder_op16 (tau 0.77, regret 12.5 %).
- Reading: the data are compatible with a modest positive link between R2 faithfulness and BOHB's gain but do not show one; the "R2 is faithful on the held-out designs" reading offered in milestones 55 and 60 is a hypothesis these 50 cells cannot confirm (tau is a whole-pool rank statistic, a KDE on the top quartile cares about the top). The paper wording should say "possibly" until a degraded-R2 experiment (needs the flow) tests it causally.
- Limits: as listed above; sample size sets the interval width (about +/-0.3), so an effect of rho 0.3 would not be detected.
- Next: causal test by degrading R2 on purpose (noise injected into the R2 features at fixed tau) and replaying BOHB, no flow needed; or the unfaithful-R2 held-out designs, which need OpenROAD.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 60: out-of-sample check of the min-observations choice (predictions written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestones 57 and 58 scored every min-obs setting on the cells it was tuned on and proposed no default for that reason. `experiments/bohb/oos_minobs.py` reports the 40 main cells and the 10 held-out cells of milestone 27 separately for min obs 3, 5, 6, 8, 12, then picks the best on the 40 and scores it on the 10 (100 paired seeds).
- **Predictions on record:** (1) at 15 equivalents the min obs chosen on the 40 cells is within 0.5 points of the best on the 10 held-out cells; (2) BOHB with that choice is below random routing on the 10 held-out cells at 15; (3) at 25 and 40 random is at or below BOHB on the 10 held-out cells for every min obs (no out-of-sample win beyond 15).
- Limits: 10 held-out cells are few and from one flow corner; all 50 cells share designs families.

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/oos_minobs.txt`; 5 min obs values x (40 + 10) cells x 100 seeds, about 8 min, no flow).** Pooled-mean regret %, BOHB (random routing) on the 10 held-out cells: at 15, min obs 3 / 5 / 6 / 8 / 12 give 0.42 / 0.64 / 0.64 / 0.64 / 1.96 (random 2.75); at 25, 0.43 to 0.49 for min obs 3 to 12 (random 1.30); at 40, 0.14 to 0.19 (random 0.32). On the 40 main cells min obs 5 is best at 15 (2.54 against random 3.56) and random wins at 40 (0.36 against 1.38 to 1.49).
  - (1) **held:** min obs 5, chosen on the 40 cells, scores 0.64 on the held-out 10 against 0.42 for the held-out best (min obs 3), 0.22 apart.
  - (2) **held:** 0.64 against random 2.75 at 15.
  - (3) **wrong:** on the 10 held-out cells BOHB is below random at 25 (0.43 to 0.49 against 1.30) and, by 0.13 to 0.18 points, at 40, while on the 40 main cells random wins at 40 by about 1 point. So the held-out designs favour BOHB at every budget, and the pooled-mean conclusion at 25 and 40 comes from the 40 main cells; the held-out set cannot overturn it (10 cells, differences at 40 are inside noise, and low regrets are floored near 0).
- Reading: the min obs choice transfers (5 to 6 stays near the held-out best), and min obs 12 is the only setting that loses the 15-equivalent win out of sample too (1.96 against 2.75 is still below, but the margin collapses). The held-out win is bigger than the main-cell win at every budget, so the BOHB result is design-set dependent in size; I do not read the 25 and 40 held-out numbers as a reversal.
- Limits: 10 held-out cells, one corner; the held-out cells were also pooled in milestones 57 and 58; no unfaithful-R2 designs (still needs OpenROAD).
- Next: unfaithful-R2 held-out designs (mult24, mult32 relaxed) under the router flow.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 59: carry milestone 58 into the paper (no flow run)
- PAPER.md section 6 item 3 now cites the min-observations grid (milestone 58): win at 15 equivalents fades gradually between min obs 8 and 12, none at 25 or 40. Next: a held-out set with an unfaithful R2 (mult24, mult32 relaxed), needs OpenROAD.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 58: min-observations grid for the BOHB low-budget win (predictions written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestone 57 found min observations is the one hyperparameter that removes the 15-equivalent win (10: 3.14 against random 3.40) and listed a finer grid as next. `experiments/bohb/minobs_grid.py` repeats the sweep at min obs 3, 5, 8, 12 (min obs 2 crashes: the bad set is empty, so it is excluded) (other settings at defaults; with 4, 6, 10 from milestone 57 this gives a 8-point grid), 50 cells pooled, 100 paired seeds.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) BOHB's pooled mean at 15 is below random's 3.40 for min obs up to 8 and above it at 12 (the threshold sits between 8 and 10); (2) min obs 3 is worse than 4 to 6 at 15 (a model fitted on too few points is noisy), so the curve is U-shaped, not monotone; (3) at 25 no min-obs value in the grid beats random (1.68) by more than 0.2 points on the pooled mean; (4) at 40 random stays below BOHB at every min obs.
- Limits: same cells for choosing and scoring; one-dimensional; not independent cells.

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/minobs_grid.txt`; min obs 3, 5, 8, 12; 50 cells, 100 seeds, about 8 min, no flow).** BOHB pooled mean regret % at 15 / 25 / 40 (random 3.40 / 1.68 / 0.35). Together with milestone 57's values (4: not tabulated here; 6: 2.22 / 1.74 / 1.13; 10: 3.14 at 15): min obs 3 2.40 / 1.49 / 1.17; 5 2.16 / 1.56 / 1.13; 8 2.59 / 1.74 / 1.18; 12 3.28 / 1.74 / 1.23.
  - (1) **mostly held, edge wrong:** BOHB is below random at 15 up to min obs 8 (2.59) and the margin shrinks to nothing as the threshold grows; at 12 it is 3.28 against 3.40, still nominally below, so the win vanishes gradually between 8 and 12, not at a sharp threshold (10 was 3.14). Differences of 0.1 are noise at this cell count.
  - (2) held weakly: min obs 3 (2.40) is worse than 5 (2.16) and 6 (2.22), so the curve has a shallow minimum around 5 to 6; the effect is 0.2 points, within what the 50 non-independent cells resolve.
  - (3) held for `bohb`: the best value at 25 is 1.49 (min obs 3), 0.19 below random, under my 0.2 bar, so no setting gives a pooled-mean win at 25 that I would call real. The `bohb_r4first` variant is 1.17 at min obs 3 to 8 (0.51 below random) but it is the variant I chose after seeing data (milestone 54), so I do not count it as confirmation.
  - (4) held: at 40 random (0.35) is below `bohb` at every min obs (1.13 to 1.23); `bohb_r4first` 0.54 is the closest.
- Reading: the 15-equivalent win is flat for min obs 3 to 8 and fades by 12; there is no knife-edge, and no setting rescues 25 or 40. Min obs 2 is invalid (empty bad set), which the script's guard does not catch: noted, not fixed, as 2 is outside any sensible range.
- Limits: scored on the cells they were chosen from; not an out-of-sample default; non-independent cells.
- Next: a held-out set with an unfaithful R2 (mult24 and mult32 relaxed under the router flow), still needs hours of flow compute and OpenROAD.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 57: how sensitive is the BOHB low-budget win to its three untuned hyperparameters? (predictions written before the sweep; no flow)
- Why: milestones 54 and 55 fixed gamma 0.25, random fraction 1/3 and min observations 6 without tuning, and say so in their limits. Before the paper leans on "KDE entrants beat random routing at low budget", check the claim is not a knife-edge of those values. `experiments/bohb/sweep.py` varies one at a time (gamma 0.1, 0.15, 0.4; random fraction 0, 0.15, 0.6; min obs 4, 10), reruns `bohb.py` on the 40 + 10 cells (100 paired seeds, same cost model), and pools to 50 cells. `bohb.py` gains env-var overrides (defaults unchanged, reproduces milestone 55's table).
- **Predictions on record:** (1) at 15 and 25 routed-run equivalents BOHB's pooled mean regret is below random routing's in at least 7 of the 8 non-base settings (the win is not knife-edge); (2) random fraction 0 is the worst setting for BOHB at 25 (no exploration, density model locks onto early noise); (3) at 40, random routing stays at or below BOHB in the pooled mean for most settings (milestone 54's pattern; the held-out 10 cells pull the pool toward BOHB, so I expect a narrow margin); (4) no setting beats the base by more than 0.5 percentage points at 15, i.e. the untuned defaults are not far from a local optimum. If (1) fails in 3 or more settings, the paper must call the win setting-dependent.
- Limits known up front: one-at-a-time, no interaction grid; the 50 cells are not independent; the settings are chosen with the same cells they are scored on, so a best setting is not an out-of-sample choice and I report the spread, not a new default.

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/sweep.txt`; 9 settings x 50 cells x 100 seeds, 9.6 min, no flow).** Pooled mean regret %, random routing 3.40 / 1.68 / 0.35 at 15 / 25 / 40; BOHB at the defaults 2.22 / 1.74 / 1.13.
  - (1) **half held.** At 15, BOHB beats random in 8 of 8 settings (2.02 to 3.14 against 3.40). At 25 it beats random in only 4 of 8 (gamma 0.1, 0.15, prand 0.6, minobs 4: 1.49 to 1.60 against 1.68) and the **defaults themselves are above random on the 50-cell pooled mean (1.74 against 1.68)**. Milestone 54's 25-equivalent claim rested on cell win counts (25 of 36), not on the mean; on the mean it was already 2.05 against 1.77 there. The paper must say the low-budget win is at 15 on the mean, and at 25 on per-cell counts only.
  - (2) held: random fraction 0 is BOHB's worst setting at 25 (2.03).
  - (3) held, but the margin is not narrow: at 40 random (0.35) beats BOHB (1.13 to 1.23) in every setting; only `bohb_r4first` (0.42 to 0.73) comes close.
  - (4) held: the best setting at 15 (gamma 0.15, 2.02) improves on the defaults by 0.20 points.
  - New: **min observations 10 removes the win at 15** (3.14 against 3.40): with a threshold of 10 the density model rarely activates inside a 15-equivalent budget. gamma 0.4 is also worse than lower values (2.46). So the claim depends on letting the model start early (min obs 4 to 6) and a small good fraction (gamma at most 0.25).
- Reading: the 15-equivalent win is robust to gamma and random fraction, sensitive to min observations, and the 25-equivalent one is not a pooled-mean result. No setting here is out-of-sample; settings were scored on the cells they were chosen from, so no new default is proposed.
- Next: a held-out set with an unfaithful R2 (needs new flow runs for mult24 / mult32 under R5); a min-obs x budget interaction grid.

## 2026-11-02 · Milestone 56: carry milestone 55 into the paper (no flow run)
- PAPER.md section 6 item 3 now cites the held-out BOHB replay (milestone 55) in place of "unconfirmed on held-out designs", with the wrong third prediction stated. Next: a held-out set with an unfaithful R2 (mult24, mult32 relaxed).

## 2026-11-01 · Milestone 55: BOHB on the five held-out designs of milestone 27 (prediction written before the run; no flow)
- Why: milestone 54's "Next" asks for a confirmation on designs the BOHB arm was not looked at on. The 10 cells of `experiments/unseen/` (CRC-32, 64-bit priority encoder, 8x8 sorting network, AES S-box, 64-bit decoder-mux; both clocks) were not among milestone 54's 40 cells, and the hyperparameters were fixed before this look. `python3 experiments/bohb/bohb.py unseen` replays the same arms, cost model, budgets (15, 25, 40), 100 paired seeds.
- **Predictions on record (confirmation criteria fixed now):** (1) BOHB's pooled mean regret is below random routing's at 15 and 25 (the milestone 54 claim); (2) BOHB beats random-entrant Hyperband in more cells than it loses at 15 and 25; (3) at 40 random routing is at or below BOHB, as before. Milestone 27 found R2 is faithful on these designs (pick regret 0 to 4% except crc32 tight 9.3%), so I expect the cheap-rung arms to do well here and the margins to be smaller than milestone 54's. If (1) fails at both budgets, the milestone 54 low-budget win was a property of the first 40 cells and the paper must say so. With 10 cells (5 designs) sign tests have almost no power: I report counts, not p-values as evidence.

- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/results_unseen.txt`, `summary_unseen.json`; 10 cells, 100 paired seeds, about 5 min).** Mean regret % at 15 / 25 / 40: random 2.75 / 1.30 / 0.32; `sh_r2` 2.34 / 1.11 / 0.99; `hb` 1.96 / 1.03 / 0.14; `bohb` 0.64 / 0.49 / 0.14; `bohb_r4first` 0.97 / 0.39 / 0.10.
  - (1) held: BOHB is below random routing at 15 and 25, winning 8 cells and losing 2 at each (p 0.109, not evidence with 5 designs).
  - (2) held: BOHB beats random-entrant Hyperband in 9 cells and loses 1 at 15 and 25.
  - (3) **wrong**: at 40, BOHB (0.14) is below random (0.32), 8 wins to 2, so the "random catches up at 40" pattern of milestone 54 did not repeat here. My expectation that the margins would be smaller was also wrong: they are larger (0.64 against 2.75 at 15, against 2.62 against 3.56 in milestone 54), most likely because R2 is faithful on these designs (milestone 27), which is what lets a density model on R2 features work.
  - Tail: BOHB's worst cell is crc32 tight (3.4 / 2.8 / 0.8 at 15 / 25 / 40), where milestone 27 found R2's pick regret was 9.3%; the R2-first bracket is still the weak spot when R2 is unfaithful. `sh_r2` stays at 6 to 7% on that cell.
- Reading: the milestone 54 low-budget win on the pooled mean replicates on five designs it was not looked at on, with the predictions fixed beforehand (two of three held). The held-out designs are R2-friendly, so this confirms the direction, not the size, and the claim stays "KDE entrants inside a Hyperband schedule beat random routing at low budget where R2 is informative". Not yet tested where R2 is unfaithful and large (mult24 and mult32 relaxed).
- Limits: five designs (10 cells, not independent), replay of a cached pool of about 50 points, hyperparameters not tuned (fixed from milestone 54), `bohb_r4first` is the variant picked after milestone 54's first table.
- Next: PAPER.md section 6 item 3 to cite milestones 54 and 55; a held-out set with an unfaithful R2.

## 2026-11-01 · Milestone 54: BOHB proper (KDE entrants inside Hyperband brackets) against never cutting on R2 and random routing (exploratory: no predictions written before the first run; a stale branch in the script was removed and the run repeated, numbers identical)
- Why: paper section 6 item 3 and milestone 23's "Next". Milestone 23 put BOHB's density ratio in a single pool; here it chooses the entrants of each Hyperband bracket (rungs R2, R3, R4; brackets starting at each, equal budget split, eta 3). The model is fitted on the highest rung with at least 6 observations (R2 features: log mapped area, log mapped period; gamma 0.25, bandwidth floor 0.3, a third of entrants random). Arms: random routing, SH from R2 (`sh_r2`), Hyperband with random entrants (`hb`), BOHB (`bohb`, brackets R2, R3, R4) and a variant with the order reversed (`bohb_r4first`, so the model sees routed observations first). Same 40 cells (rung_bias, large, nonarith; both clocks), cost model and regret as milestone 23, budgets 15, 25, 40 routed-run equivalents, 100 paired seeds. `experiments/bohb/bohb.py`, about 80 s, runs no flow.
- **Outcome (`experiments/bohb/results.txt`).** Mean regret % over 40 cells at 15 / 25 / 40: random 3.56 / 1.77 / 0.36; `sh_r2` 4.93 / 3.72 / 3.54; `hb` 3.49 / 2.23 / 1.41; `bohb` 2.62 / 2.05 / 1.38; `bohb_r4first` 3.03 / 1.37 / 0.65.
  - BOHB beats random-entrant Hyperband in 27 / 20 / 14 cells and loses in 9 / 8 / 4 (sign-test p 0.004 / 0.036 / 0.031), so the density model is what helps within the bracket schedule.
  - BOHB beats random routing at 15 and 25 (26 and 25 wins, 10 and 11 losses, p 0.011 and 0.029) and not at 40 (21 / 15, p 0.41), where random's mean (0.36) is below BOHB's (1.38). It is the first multi-fidelity arm in the programme to beat random routing at a low budget on the pooled mean; the earlier ridge, GP and TPE arms (milestones 20 to 23) did not. The cells are not independent (each design appears at two clocks and the multiplier widths are one family), so the p values are indicative, not confirmatory.
  - The tail is not fixed: BOHB's worst cell is 15.9 / 14.3 / 13.8 % (barrel64 relaxed, barrel64 tight, mult32 relaxed), against 15.1 / 9.4 / 2.5 % for random. The R2-first bracket still cuts the optimum on those designs.
  - Putting the R4 bracket first (`bohb_r4first`) is better than R2 first at 25 and 40 (1.37 and 0.65), consistent with the model needing routed observations early; I chose this variant after seeing the first table, so it counts as a second look.
- Reading: the programme's negative headline ("no model-based arm beats never cutting or random routing") is too strong for the low-budget end: a KDE sampler inside a Hyperband schedule beats random routing at 15 and 25 routed-run equivalents on the pooled mean. At 40 the pool (about 50 points) is nearly exhausted by random routing and nothing beats it. The result is a replay of a cached pool, not a search of the 400-sequence space.
- Limits: exploratory; hyperparameters (gamma, bandwidth floor, random fraction, min observations, eta) fixed and untuned; two features; pooled-mean advantage comes from cells with large random-routing regret at 15 (the worst-cell column does not improve); no multiple-comparison correction across arms; non-independent cells; costs are table means; one corner, global-route reference.
- Next: a confirmation on a held-out design set with the prediction written first (e.g. BOHB below random routing on the pooled mean at 15 and 25 on new designs); mult24 and mult32 under R5.

## 2026-11-01 · Milestone 52: does the proof-success table (milestone 5) survive a Yosys upgrade, 0.33 to 0.70? (plan written before the run)
- Why: milestones 47 to 51 showed tool version moves routed rankings more than the router does; the proof table (SAT miter, `sat -verify -prove-asserts`) was last run on apt Yosys 0.33 and the log's "Next" lists it under the new tools. `experiments/proof_success/proof_run.py` unchanged, `--out results_y070.jsonl`, 10 s timeout, same 60 sequences, 15 designs; Yosys 0.70 from the nixpkgs cache. Analysis: per design proved<=1/3/10 s counts and median t_sat against `results.txt`, plus agreement of the proved-within-3s label per (design, sequence) between versions.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) no counter-example on any point (rewrites stay equivalent); (2) the design-level picture holds: all designs except mult7 to mult10 prove 50/50 within 1 s, mult9 and mult10 time out at 10 s on all points; (3) the per-point label (proved within 3 s) agrees with 0.33 on at least 95% of points; the mult7/mult8 boundary cells are where it may differ; (4) the number of ABC failures is unchanged (130 timeouts is a proof count, not an ABC one).
- **Outcome (`experiments/proof_success/compare_y070.txt`, `results_y070.jsonl`; 900 launched, 751 ok, 751 shared with the 0.33 table; 491 s on 4 cores).**
  - (1) held: 0 counter-examples, 627 proved, 124 timed out at 10 s.
  - (2) held: every design except mult7 to mult10 proves 50/50 within 1 s on both versions; mult10 times out on all 50 under both; mult9 proves 1 point within 10 s under 0.70 (0 before), mult8 15 against 17, mult7 0/49/50 against 0/50/50 within 1/3/10 s.
  - (3) held: the proved-within-3-s label agrees on 750 of 751 points (0.999); the one difference is a mult7 point just over 3 s. Median proof times move by under 10%.
  - (4) the ABC failure count is not identical: 751 usable points against 752 (one more sequence failed under 0.70); not investigated. Median netlist size differs by a few cells on most designs (alu8 -12.5, mult10 +24), so the rewritten netlists are not byte-identical across versions even though proof outcomes are.
- Reading: unlike routed rankings (milestones 47, 48), the proof-success table is stable across a Yosys 0.33 to 0.70 upgrade, so the milestone 5 reading (proof time is set by design, not predictable from sequence features) is not an artefact of the old solver. `proof_run.py` now reads both `stat` formats.
- Limits: same designs, 10 s timeout, same SAT solver family in Yosys; boundary cells (mult7 to mult9) are few; the H5 predictor was not refit on the new table (not needed given 0.999 label agreement).
- Next: mult24 and mult32 under R5 (hours of compute).

## 2026-11-01 · Milestone 51: is global-route "routed" (R4) still a faithful stand-in for detail routing on the larger designs? (milestone 9's set; predictions written before the run finished)
- Why: milestones 47 and 49 covered the five small designs only; "Next" since then names the larger-design set under R5. `experiments/detail_route/run.py --large --points ../large/results.jsonl --tag tight --per-design 24` re-measures R1 to R4 and adds detail routing + OpenRCX (R5) for mult16, adder_op64 and adder_ripple64 at the tight clock (mult24 and mult32 dropped: a first launch showed 220 to 280 s per mult16 detail-route with four jobs, so the two bigger multipliers would take many hours; the 4 mult16 points from that launch were discarded and rerun), the first 24 usable sequences per design (detail routing of the multipliers is slow, so pools are 24, not 50). OpenROAD 26Q3, Yosys 0.70 from the nixpkgs cache, as in milestones 47 to 49. `analyse.py tight ../large/results.jsonl _large` writes `results_large.txt`.
- **Predictions on record:** (1) tau-b(R4,R5) is at least 0.8 on mult16 and adder_op64 (the small-design median was 0.84 and 0.85); (2) R4's pick costs under 2% against R5 on both of those; (3) extracted detail-route slack is more optimistic than the global-route estimate on every point; (4) the ripple adder (few distinct scores) gives a tau that is uninformative, so it is reported but not counted in (1).
- Limits known up front: pools of 24 give wide tau intervals; tight clock only; one corner; the tool-version change is confounded with the reference change in the drift row; no multiple-comparison correction.
- **Outcome (`experiments/detail_route/results_large.txt`: 72 launched, 71 ok, 0 DRC violations, 33.9 s median detail-route; OpenROAD 26Q3, Yosys 0.70).**
  - tau-b of R4 against the detail-routed R5: adder_op64 0.93, mult16 0.93, adder_ripple64 1.00 (only 6 distinct R5 scores, so uninformative). **Prediction 1 held** (both above 0.8). R4's pick costs 0.0% on all three against R5 (prediction 2 held), and the R5 optimum is in R4's top 5 on all three.
  - R2 (mapped, no wires) against R5: tau 0.80 (adder_op64), 0.67 (mult16); its pick regret is 12.0% on adder_op64 and 3.9% on mult16, so the "R2 is biased at the tight clock" reading survives a detail-routed reference on the larger designs too. R1 tau 0.63 and 0.18.
  - Detail-route slack is more optimistic than the global-route estimate on every one of 71 points (median +0.39 ns, range +0.08 to +1.26; prediction 3 held), larger than on the small designs (+0.19).
  - Tool-version drift of R4 against the cached 26Q2 table: tau 0.70 (adder_op64), 0.81 (ripple), 0.31 (mult16): again large on the multiplier.
- Reading: global-route "routed" is a faithful reference for detail routing on mult16 and the 64-bit adders as on the five small designs, so the halving results on the larger set are not an artefact of stopping at global route. mult24 and mult32 are untested (a single mult16 detail route took 220 to 280 s with four jobs).
- Limits: three designs, pools of 24 (wide intervals), tight clock, one corner, tool-version drift confounds tool version with reference; no multiple-comparison correction; predictions 1 to 3 were written before the results were read.
- Next: mult24 and mult32 under R5 (hours of compute); the proof-success table under the new tools.

Five designs (alu8, mult8, popcount16, barrel16, mac4), tight clock only, about 250 points, because detail routing costs about 20 s per point.
- Analysis fixed now (`analyse.py`): Kendall tau-b of R1 to R4 against R5 per design, pick regret of the R2 and R4 argmin against the R5 optimum, whether the R5 optimum is in R4's top 5, the slack shift between global-route estimate and extracted detail-route parasitics, and tool-version drift of R4 against the cached table for the same points.

If (1) fails, the programme's reference rung is itself biased and every regret in the paper is regret against the wrong target.

So global-route "routed" is a mostly faithful but not exact reference; regret against it is a good, imperfect proxy.

Tool-version drift reorders the pool more than the choice of router does. I cannot separate Yosys from OpenROAD effects: both changed.

## 2026-10-31 · Milestone 45: does the rank displacement rho/k grow with pool size? (exploratory; no predictions were written before the script ran)
- Why: milestone 44's "Next". `experiments/theory/optimum_rank_n.py` (no flow run, a few seconds) subsamples each of the 20 cached r1-start cells to n' = 20, 30, 40, 50 candidates (200 subsamples each, the optimum recomputed on the subsample, keeps n'//3, n'//9, n'//27 floored at 1) and takes the worst rho_j/k_j over the three cuts.
- Outcome (`optimum_rank_n_results.txt`): median rho/k 2.33, 2.60, 3.00, 3.00 and mean 4.2, 5.7, 7.2, 8.9 at n' = 20, 30, 40, 50; the share of subsamples with rho/k <= 1 falls 0.168, 0.130, 0.086, 0.050 and the share <= 2 falls 0.475, 0.474, 0.414, 0.300. So the zero-regret assumption D(1) gets harder as the pool grows, not easier.
- Reading: this is partly built in. The last cut keeps k = 1 at every n' tested, so its ratio is the raw count rho_3, which can only grow with the pool, while the earlier cuts' k grow with n'. The result says the assumption cannot be expected to hold on larger pools; it does not say how fast rho/k grows beyond 50.
- Limits: pools are capped at the 50 to 52 cached candidates, so nothing above n' = 50 is measured; subsamples of one table overlap, so the 200 draws per cell are not independent; no tests (script only); the two clocks and ten designs are 20 cells, not 20 independent designs.
- Next: a pool above 52 candidates needs new flow runs (OpenROAD); until then rho/k at larger n is untested.

## 2026-10-30 · Milestone 44: how far do the rungs displace the pool optimum, and is a rank assumption on it enough for zero regret? (exploratory; no predictions were written before the script ran)
- Why: milestone 43 said a pilot-side guarantee needs an assumption on the optimum's own excess. The simplest one is rank displacement: x* is cut at a cut keeping k only if at least k rivals rank at or ahead of it, so if rho_j (rivals at or ahead of x* under cut j's score) is below k_j at every cut, regret is exactly 0 (the kappa = 0 case of milestone 39). `experiments/theory/optimum_rank.py` (no flow run, about 5 s) measures rho_j / k_j on the 40 cached cells and checks the implication over 200 tie-breaks each.
- Outcome (`optimum_rank_results.txt`): the implication holds (0 of the 11 guaranteed cells shows any regret). Cells whose worst rho/k is at most 1, 2 and 4: 13, 21 and 29 of 40; median 1.97. Where rho/k > 1, only 0.167 of tie-breaks reach zero regret on average.
- Reading: the assumption "x* ranks inside the survivors at every rung" is a guarantee in 11 of 40 cells and nearly one in three at most; it does not hold generally, and failing it by a factor 2 already loses the zero-regret outcome in most draws. The optimum is typically within about 2 survivor-widths of the cut line, which is why halving sees non-zero regret in the other cells. A usable pilot-side assumption would bound rho/k from above by a constant and then bound the excess among those rivals, which is the quantity milestone 43 showed cannot be got from a quantile.
- Limits: one table and tool version; x* is the pool optimum, so a pilot cannot compute rho (it is not observed without the full table); no tests (script only).
- Next: model rho/k across pool sizes (does it grow with n?) or detail routing, which needs OpenROAD.

## 2026-10-30 · Milestone 43: no quantile of the per-candidate excess g bounds halving regret in general (a counterexample; theory, no flow run)
- Why: milestones 41 and 42 found the q-quantile of g covers empirically except in cells where the pool optimum itself has a large g. This asks whether any q < 1 can be made to work as a theorem. `experiments/theory/quantile_counterexample.py` builds the smallest instance: one cut to n/3 survivors, the optimum (y = 1) ranked last by the rung, every other candidate at y = c ranked by index. Every other candidate has only equal-y rivals ahead of it, so g = 0 for all but the optimum, whose g is log c.
- Outcome (`quantile_counterexample_results.txt`; `tests/test_bound.py::test_no_quantile_of_g_bounds_halving_regret`, n = 30, 90, 300): the optimum is cut at the first cut, so realised regret is log c (0.693 for c = 2, 2.303 for c = 10), while the 0.5, 0.9 and 0.95 quantiles of g are 0 for every n tried (30 to 3000); the 0.99 quantile is 0 once n >= 100. In general the q-quantile is 0 whenever q <= 1 - 1/n, so for any fixed q < 1 and large n the claim "regret <= sum of the cut quantiles of g" is false.
- Reading: this closes the branch from 41 and 42. The empirical coverage (97.6% of pilot draws at q = 0.95) is a property of this table, where the optimum is rarely an outlier in g; it cannot become a certificate by choosing q or the pilot size, because the failure instance puts all of the regret on one candidate. Only q = 1 (the maximum, milestone 37's bound) is valid, and the pilot cannot certify a maximum (milestones 38 and 40). A pilot-side regret guarantee needs an assumption that bounds the optimum's own excess, not a better statistic.
- Limits: a constructed instance, not a claim that real tables look like it (the real table has 2 failing cells of 40 at q = 0.9); one cut; it shows quantiles cannot be a theorem, not that they are useless as a diagnostic.
- Next: state an assumption on the optimum's excess (for example, bounded rank-displacement of the optimum under the rung) that a pilot can check, or detail routing, which needs OpenROAD.

## 2026-10-30 · Milestone 42: does a quantile of g computed inside a 16-point pilot bound halving regret? (exploratory; no predictions were written before the script ran)
- Why: milestone 41 left a pilot-side version open. `experiments/theory/quantile_g_pilot.py` (no flow run, about 1 minute) draws 300 random 16-point pilots per cell, computes g^(k') on the pilot alone (rivals inside the pilot, k' = max(1, round(k x 16 / n))), takes the q-quantile over pilot candidates, sums over cuts, and compares with the realised full-pool regret of optimum-seeking halving (40 cells x 200 tie-breaks).
- Outcome (`quantile_g_pilot_results.txt`): share of (pilot, tie-break) draws with regret above the pilot bound: q = 0.5 0.523, q = 0.9 0.0762, q = 0.95 0.0242. Cells where more than 20% of draws violate: 23, 5 and 1 of 40. The pilot bound is under the spread of y in over half the pilots in 40, 37 and 36 cells. Median pilot / full-table bound: 0.01, 1.05, 1.02.
- Reading: at q = 0.95 a 16-point pilot gives a bound that holds in 97.6% of draws and says something (is under the spread) in 36 of 40 cells, using no full table; at q = 0.9 the violation share is 7.6%, above 5%. This is empirical coverage on one table, not a certificate: the same 1 cell fails as in milestone 41, and the pilot quantile is not proved to dominate the optimum's g.
- Limits: one table and tool version; pilot size, q grid and k scaling fixed once; the pilot is iid from the pool (pool-wide, not the 8 disjoint pairs of milestone 36); no tests (script only).
- Next: a proof or a counter-example for the quantile bound, or detail routing, which needs OpenROAD.

## 2026-10-30 · Milestone 41: does a quantile of the per-candidate excess g_x^(k) bound halving regret? (predictions fixed before the script ran)
- Why: milestone 40 showed no fixed multiplier turns a 16-point pilot estimate of iota^(k) (a maximum over candidates) into a bound. The extreme-value alternative: replace the maximum by the q-quantile Q_q of g_x^(k) over candidates x (g_x^(k) = k-th largest of max(0, log y(z) - log y(x)) over rivals z ranked at or ahead of x). A pilot of m iid candidates sees a value at or above Q_q with probability 1 - q^m (0.81 for q = 0.9, m = 16), so a quantile is the thing a pilot could certify. `experiments/theory/quantile_g.py` (no flow run) uses the full table to ask whether sum_j Q_q(g^(k_j)) bounds the realised regret of the optimum-seeking halving, over the 40 group x start cells and 200 tie-breaks, for q in {0.5, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95}.
- **Predictions (fixed now):** (1) At q = 0.9 realised regret exceeds the quantile bound in more than 5% of (cell, tie-break) draws, because the pool optimum is selected, not sampled (the winner's curse of milestone 4 again), so a quantile of g over all candidates under-describes g at x*. (2) The share of cells with a violation falls with q but is still above 0 at q = 0.95. (3) The q = 0.9 bound is under the spread of y in more cells than the max bound (32). Each is refuted by the opposite.
- Limits known up front: idealised (rivals come from the full table, not from the pilot); one table and tool version; a quantile bound is probabilistic at best and is not a theorem about the argmin.

### Outcome (`experiments/theory/quantile_g.py`, about 3 s, `quantile_g_results.txt`; 40 cells x 200 tie-breaks)
- Share of draws with realised regret above the quantile bound: q = 0.5 0.511, 0.75 0.286, 0.9 0.0500, 0.95 0.0250. Cells with any violation: 21, 12, 2, 1 of 40. The q = 0.9 bound is under the spread of y in 38 cells (the max bound: 32).
- **(1) refuted, narrowly:** the violation share at q = 0.9 is 0.0500 exactly (400 of 8000), not above 5%. **(2) held:** one cell still fails at q = 0.95. **(3) held:** 38 against 32 non-vacuous cells.
- Reading: the quantile bound is much tighter than the maximum and almost always holds, but its failures are whole cells, not unlucky draws: the two violating cells (tight adder_op16 starting at R2, relaxed mac4 starting at R1) fail on all 200 tie-breaks, because in them the pool optimum is itself one of the few candidates with a large g, so a quantile over all candidates misses it. That is the winner's-curse mechanism predicted, but it bites in 2 to 3 cells of 40, not broadly. The 1 - q^m coverage of a pilot maximum is about candidates drawn at random, and the argmin is not one, so this is not a certificate; it is evidence that a pilot-side certificate anchored to the optimum (not to a random candidate) is what is missing.
- Limits: idealised (rivals from the full table, not the pilot); one table and tool version; q chosen on a grid of four; no pilot-side version run; no tests (script only).
- Next: a pilot-side version (g computed from pilot rivals, quantile of the pilot's own g, and the share of cells where it covers), or detail routing, which needs OpenROAD.

## 2026-10-29 · Milestone 40: can a fixed multiplier turn the 16-point pilot estimate of iota^(k) into a bound? (negative; exploratory, no predictions written before the script ran)
- Why: milestone 38 left the pilot-side regret certificate open, with "an inflation whose validity is not proved". `experiments/theory/kth_inflate.py` (about 5 s, no flow run) reuses milestone 38's 55 cells and seeded pilots and asks what multiplier c makes c x (pilot estimate) >= the full-table iota^(k) in 80% of draws, and whether one c chosen on other designs transfers to a held-out design.
- Outcome (`kth_inflate_results.txt`): coverage >= 0.8 in 0 / 6 / 18 / 35 / 44 / 49 of 55 cells for c = 1 / 1.25 / 1.5 / 2 / 3 / 5. Per-cell multiplier for 80%: median 1.75, p90 5.79, and no finite multiplier in 2 cells (pilot value 0 in over 20% of draws). Leave-one-design-out: the multiplier picked on the other nine designs (3 to 5) covers 44 of 55 held-out cells; per design it fails in 3 of 6 cells for adder_op16 and crc16, 2 of 6 for fifo8x4 and mac4, and the single adder_ripple16 cell.
- Reading: a fixed inflation does not give a bound. The factor needed spans 1 to above 5 across cells and the pilot can be exactly 0, so any certificate from this estimator would need a model of how the k-th rival excess grows with pool size, not a constant. The regret bound stays a full-table diagnostic; the paper's claim is unchanged.
- Limits: empirical coverage over 300 pilots per cell on one table and tool version; the grid of multipliers is coarse; no proof either way; no tests (script only).
- Next: the paper's theory section with iota^(k) stated as a full-table diagnostic, or an extreme-value argument for the pilot maximum.

- Why: `PAPER.md` section 6 item 4 still described the milestone 35 state (disjoint pairs unproved) and did not mention the proved independent-pairs pilot (milestone 36, coverage 0.826 or more over 60 cells) or the failed pilot estimate of iota^(k) (milestone 38, median 0.85 of the true value, no coverage statement). Section 4 already carried milestones 37 and 39.
